Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n79\t- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n80\t- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n81\t- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n82\t- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n83\t- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n84\t- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n85\t- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n86\t- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n87\t- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n88\t- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n89\t- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n90\t- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n91\t- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n92\t- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n93\t- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n94\t- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n95\t- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n96\t- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n97\t- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n98\t- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n99\t- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n100\t- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n101\t- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n102\t- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n103\t- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n104\t- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n105\t- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n106\t- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n107\t- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n108\t- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n109\t- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n110\t- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n111\t- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n112\t- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n113\t- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n114\t- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n\n## Task Handoffs\n\n- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n- Later geometry, material, source, and monitor tasks can plug concrete tagged models into the existing container fields without changing the ordered-container semantics.\n- `task-004` and `task-005` can reuse `ComponentIR.payload` plus the shared IR schema version constants instead of inventing another transport shape.\n- Later geometry, source, monitor, boundary, and grid tasks should route new object models through `autofdtd.core.validation.normalize_component()` or extend its equivalents instead of scattering policy checks across each family.\n- Later compiler work can rely on wrapper IR bounds as conservative planning envelopes; exact voxelization or boolean rasterization still belongs in scene compilation rather than the public geometry layer.\n- Later compiler work can consume `StructureIR.source_index` plus `StructureIR.precedence_rank` directly when building ordered region materialization plans or conflict diagnostics.\n- Later grid/compiler tasks should treat `LayerRefinementSpecIR` as planning metadata only; corner finding, snapping-point localization, and structure-derived meshing still need a separate scene-aware meshing pass.\n- Material and compiler tasks should consume `SubpixelSpecIR.averaging_targets` and `requires_anisotropic_materialization` directly instead of re-deriving which interface families need tensor-valued effective media.\n- Later runtime and chunk-planning tasks can treat `IsotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus `ConstitutiveMode` as the stable baseline constitutive contract before dispersive and anisotropic state is added.\n- Later Lorentz, Drude, Debye, and Sellmeier tasks should lower onto the `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` auxiliary-state contract whenever the family can be reduced to pole-residue terms instead of creating parallel runtime state layouts.\n- Later subpixel materialization work can target `AnisotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus the diagonal anisotropic kernel path instead of inventing a separate   cell_sizes=(float(cell_sizes[index]),),\n                centers=(float(centers[index]),),\n            )\n\n        lower, upper = _bracket_indices(centers, center)\n        if lower == upper:\n            return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n                indices=(lower,),\n                weights=(1.0,),\n                cell_sizes=(float(cell_sizes[lower]),),\n                centers=(float(centers[lower]),),\n            )\n        left_center = centers[lower]\n        right_center = centers[upper]\n        fraction = float((center - left_center) / (right_center - left_center))\n        return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n            indices=(lower, upper),\n            weights=(1.0 - fraction, fraction),\n            cell_sizes=(float(cell_sizes[lower]), float(cell_sizes[upper])),\n            centers=(float(left_center), float(right_center)),\n        )\n\n    lower = center - size / 2.0\n    upper = center + size / 2.0\n    if confine_to_bounds:\n        overlaps = np.minimum(boundaries[1:], upper) - np.maximum(boundaries[:-1], lower)\n        mask = overlaps > tolerance\n        if not np.any(mask):\n            raise ValueError(\"UniformCurrentSource support does not overlap the resolved grid\")\n        weights = overlaps[mask] / cell_sizes[mask]\n        indices = np.nonzero(mask)[0]\n    else:\n        mask = (centers >= lower - tolerance) & (centers <= upper + tolerance)\n        if not np.any(mask):\n            raise ValueError(\"UniformCurrentSource support does not include any resolved grid cells\")\n        weights = np.ones(int(np.count_nonzero(mask)), dtype=float)\n        indices = np.nonzero(mask)[0]\n    return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n        indices=tuple(int(index) for index in indices),\n        weights=tuple(float(weight) for weight in weights),\n        cell_sizes=tuple(float(cell_sizes[index]) for index in indices),\n        centers=tuple(float(centers[index]) for index in indices),\n    )\n\n\ndef _transverse_extent(source: UniformCurrentSource, grid: ResolvedGrid) -> float:\n    placements = []\n    axes = (grid.x, grid.y, grid.z)\n    for axis in range(3):\n        if axis == source.component_axis:\n            continue\n        extent = source.size[axis]\n        if extent > 0.0:\n            placements.append(extent)\n            continue\n        centers = _axis_centers(axes[axis])\n        cell_sizes = _axis_sizes(axes[axis])\n        index = int(np.argmin(np.abs(centers - source.center[axis])))\n        placements.append(float(cell_sizes[index]))\n    measure = 1.0\n    for extent in placements:\n        measure *= extent\n    return measure\n\n\ndef compile_uniform_current_source(\n    source: UniformCurrentSource,\n    *,\n    grid: _compile_axis_placement(\n            axis_grid,\n            center=source.center[axis],\n            size=source.size[axis],\n            interpolate=source.interpolate,\n            confine_to_bounds=source.confine_to_bounds,\n        )\n        for axis, axis_grid in enumerate((grid.x, grid.y, grid.z))\n    )\n\n    placements: list[tuple[int, int, int]] = []\n    placement_weights: list[float] = []\n    for x_index, x_weight in zip(\n        axis_placements[0].indices,\n        axis_placements[0].weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        for y_index, y_weight in zip(\n            axis_placements[1].indices,\n            axis_placements[1].weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for z_index, z_weight in zip(\n                axis_placements[2].indices,\n                axis_placements[2].weights,\n                strict=True,\n            ):\n                placements.append((x_index, y_index, z_index))\n                placement_weights.append(float(x_weight * y_weight * z_weight))\n\n    amplitude_scale = 1.0\n    if source.current_amplitude_definition == \"total\":\n        transverse_extent = _transverse_extent(source, grid)\n        if transverse_extent <= 0.0:\n            raise ValueError(\"UniformCurrentSource total-current normalization needs positive extent\")\n        amplitude_scale = 1.0 / transverse_extent\n\n    return CompiledUniformCurrentSource(\n        source=source,\n        field_kind=source.field_kind,\n        component_axis=source.component_axis,\n        placement_kind=source.placement_kind,\n        placements=tuple(placements),\n        placement_weights=tuple(placement_weights),\n        axis_placements=axis_placements,\n The Meep paper sharpens this further: the solver is designed to preserve an illusion of continuous space and time on top of chunked discrete execution, uses pervasive interpolation and restriction for sources, monitors, and field queries, relies on anisotropic subpixel smoothing to remove first-order interface error, and favors bulk region operations over pointwise APIs. High-value references: `../meep/doc/docs/Parallel_Meep.md`, `../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`, `../meep/src/structure.cpp`, `../meep/src/boundaries.cpp`, `../meep/src/fields.cpp`, `../meep/src/loop_in_chunks.cpp`, `../meep/src/near2far.cpp`, and `papers/meep_paper.pdf`.\n65\t\n66\t### Reference 5\n67\t\n68\tWarp backend findings: Warp kernels are Python-authored and JIT-compiled per module; all kernels in a module compile together and should be stabilized early to avoid reload churn. Arrays, vectors, matrices, and structs are strong fits for field buffers and metadata. CUDA launches are asynchronous; streams and events are first-class; graph capture and replay are realistic optimization targets for timestep loops; tile APIs are promising for later shared-memory optimizations but should not block a first Phase 1 backend; and built-in profiling via ScopedTimer, NVTX, and CUDA activity summaries is good enough for Phase 1 instrumentation. Benchmarking guidance from `papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf` adds that FDTD should be tracked primarily in cells updated per second or Gcells/s, usually as a bandwidth-bound workload, and that benchmark metadata must preserve grid shape, total cells, timestep count, precision, boundary or material class, single-versus-multi-GPU context, and separate end-to-end versus steady-state timing.\n69\t\n70\t### Reference 6\n71\t\n72\tScheduling findings from `papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`: a naive GPU FDTD is constrained mostly by memory traffic and synchronization, so the important architectural seam is the split between chunk-local interior work, lower-dimensional boundary exchange, and a pluggable execution policy that may later delay or overlap neighbor consumption. Phase 1 should remain bulk-synchronous by default, but chunk metadata and runtime orchestration should already preserve per-face, per-stage halo semantics, interior-versus-boundary planning, and scheduler hooks so future systolic or overlapped execution can be added without redesign.\n73\t\n74\t### Reference 7\n75\t\n76\tGeometry and mode-solver findings: GeometryPrimitives.jl is centered on analytic shape queries such as level, nearest surface point, normals, bounds, and translation rather than a full scene graph. Orthogonal prisms and convex polygons are natural early scope; subpixel support exists mainly through local plane-cut overlap ideas rather than a full arbitrary-shape averaging engine; KD-tree and periodic replication patterns matter early; and libctl provides richer object-model and overlap-integral precedents for material-carrying ordered geometry lists and periodic duplication. VectorModesolver.jl is best treated as a formulation and interface reference rather than a polished dependency surface. It expects a sampled tensor-valued permittivity callback over a rectilinear cross-section, assembles a sparse operator, solves for magnetic fields, and reconstructs electric fields.\n77\t\n78\t### Reference 8\n79\t\n80\tWorking-memory seed facts that later tasks should preserve: chunk is the architectural unit while rank or device is placement; stored topology and logical topology diverge because of symmetry and periodic or Bloch remapping; scene compilation must remain distinct from stepping; continuous problem specifications must lower through explicit interpolation and restriction into discrete execution artifacts; geometry lowering and materialization must precede serious kernels; subpixel smoothing can induce effective anisotropy even for physically isotropic materials; stage-specific halos and monitor metadata should be explicit; Tidy3D-style compatibility should be represented as a tagged immutable IR with normalization rules and unsupported-feature policy; runtime metrics should include Gcells/s plus separate end-to-end and steady-state timing; and near-to-far should be built on top of DFT monitor infrastructure rather than as an isolated feature.\n81\t\n82\t\n83\t## Runtime Context\n84\t\n85\t- The runner injects the current task's title, success criteria, and notes from `loop.spec.md` on every iteration.
